4:T852,No new algorithms are proposed; the core contribution is the integration of existing distributed training components (FSDP/ZeRO-3, dataset sharding, checkpoint fault tolerance, hardware telemetry) into a B300-adapted operational discipline, delivering four reusable artifacts:
1. Telemetry-based triage system: Abandons GPU utilization, uses B300-calibrated board power bands combined with three dimensions — cross-rank symmetry and duration — to rapidly classify compute (~700W+), communication (~640W, uniform power across all ranks), data starvation (~575W, scattered cross-rank power), checkpoint/deadlock (~190W; checkpoint only drops power on rank 0 and recovers within 2 minutes, deadlock shows zero power on one rank with full load on others and no recovery), and idle states. It is paired with a one-line monitoring command and decision workflow, requiring no profiler attachment to locate faults.
2. Controlled negative results: Validates that three common optimizations are ineffective at the 16-GPU 32B full fine-tuning scale: local NVMe caching brings no throughput gain over NFS (the bottleneck is tokenization and multi-tenant CPU contention, not storage media), enabling gradient checkpointing reduces throughput by 1.6x (recomputation is pure waste given B300’s sufficient memory), and corrects an earlier misdiagnosis of a power anomaly.
3. Calibrated scaling reference: Provides full-epoch throughput, GPU-hour, and memory footprint measurements for 4/8/16-GPU B300 setups.
4. Failure case and hardening: Identifies an end-of-epoch NCCL deadlock caused by packing imbalance (unequal block counts across ranks after packing; ranks that finish first enter the next collective communication wait early, triggering a permanent deadlock) — a scenario not covered by PyTorch Join or drop_last. Proposes a hardening scheme consisting of a 2.7-second preflight gate (verify all ranks have identical block counts before launch, truncate to the minimum otherwise) and an external watcher (monitor power and throughput anomalies to trigger automatic restart), and accounts for the compute cost of silent deadlocks.5:T48c,The testbed is a 32.76B-parameter dense model (Qwen3-32B) trained on 2 nodes with 16 NVIDIA B300 GPUs, using FSDP FULL_SHARD (ZeRO-3). Training config: sequence length 2048, micro-batch size 1, gradient accumulation steps 8, global batch size 128 sequences (262,144 tokens/step), ~3,854 steps per epoch, AdamW with cosine warmup, lr=1e-5, bf16 mixed precision, gradient checkpointing disabled.
Key measured data:
- Memory: Per-GPU model state is 32.8GB, total footprint including activations is 82GB, 30% of B300’s 275GB memory;
- Throughput: Disabled gradient checkpointing is 1.6x faster than enabled;
- Failure cost: An undetected packing imbalance deadlock led to 12 hours of no output, corresponding to 192 wasted GPU-hours;
- Data pipeline: Without packing, throughput collapsed from 52k tok/s to 21k tok/s under multi-tenant NFS load; packing eliminated this fluctuation (core benefit is determinism, not throughput gain);
- Scaling efficiency: Full-epoch measured GPU-hours for 4/8/16-GPU setups are provided as a budget reference for similar-scale tasks.
All conclusions are validated via controlled对照 experiments to eliminate confounding variables.6:T45e,1. First read Section 3 (Telemetry-Based Triage) carefully: focus on the power band table and the symmetry distinction logic between deadlock and checkpoint, which is the most directly reusable operational tool.
2. Next prioritize Section 5 (Packing Imbalance Deadlock Case): understand the boundaries of existing distributed tools and the hardening logic of preflight gate + watcher, a hidden problem commonly encountered in large-batch training.
3. Section 4 (Negative Results): Cross-check against your own training optimization checklist to avoid ineffective optimizations on B300 and similar large-memory accelerators.
4. Section 6 (Calibrated Scaling Reference): Use directly as a reference table for hardware selection and budget estimation.
5. Skip Section 8 (Related Work); no algorithmic innovation is presented in this report.
Deep dive directions: Extend the power triage logic to calibration for other hardware (e.g., H100, H200), or integrate with DCGM for automated fault detection; the packing imbalance problem can be extended to consistency verification workflow design for pretraining data sharding.7:T5c8,The pipeline has two stages. First, the authors estimate latent task difficulty from observed agent-task outcomes. Each trial is treated as a Bernoulli observation, forming a partially observed binary response matrix over agents and tasks. They fit a one-parameter logistic IRT model, or Rasch model, with one scalar ability parameter θ_a per agent configuration and one scalar difficulty parameter β_t per task. This is preferable to raw pass rate because difficulty is inferred relative to a population of agents on a shared scale, making it more suitable for cross-benchmark comparison.

Second, they train predictors of β_t using only pre-rollout features. These include textual task descriptions, environment and action-space descriptions, metadata, and uncertainty-related signals such as entropy that can be obtained without full interaction. A major methodological contribution is the evaluation protocol. The paper argues that response AUC can look strong even when task-level difficulty estimates are poor, so it emphasizes rank-based metrics such as Spearman correlation for judging whether the predictor actually learns difficulty ordering. The residual analysis is also important: discrepancies between predicted and observed IRT difficulty are used diagnostically. Large positive residuals often indicate hidden hardness factors such as infeasible environments or broken evaluators, while large negative residuals may reveal familiarity, templating, or shortcuts.8:T525,The empirical foundation is substantial. The main analysis subset contains 5,230 tasks from 17 benchmarks, evaluated by 497 agent configurations built from 216 models and 90 scaffolds. The full corpus includes 415,470 agent-task outcomes. Domains span coding and SWE, function calling, web navigation, computer use, terminal tasks, math, ML, cybersecurity, and general-purpose suites. Task dates range from 2010 to 2026, and each agent-task pair has about 1.2 trials on average.

The headline numbers are straightforward. Under K-fold evaluation, the combined feature set reaches Spearman ρ = 0.399, significantly better than the baseline at p < 0.001. Under out-of-distribution evaluation, it reaches ρ = 0.225. Among single feature families, entropy performs best. The paper also shows that response AUC can remain high even when task-level difficulty ranking is weak, which supports the claim that rank-based evaluation is the more faithful metric. The residual case studies are especially useful: some tasks predicted to be moderate turn out to be much harder because the environment is infeasible or the verifier is faulty, while others that look difficult in text are actually easy due to familiarity or shortcuts. That makes the predictor useful not only for scoring tasks, but also for debugging benchmarks.9:T4d8,CoBa formulates test-time reasoning as a unified compute allocation problem with four action types: candidate generation, lightweight verification, strong verification, and stopping. Two cost metrics are defined: total token cost (sum of input and output tokens across all model calls) and parameter-weighted token cost (tokens scaled by model parameter count to reflect relative compute of different model sizes). The staged routing policy first uses a small fixed warm-up budget to generate a diverse candidate pool, then scores all candidates with cheap verification (answer frequency + small model judge scores). Based on these scores, the system decides to stop, sample more candidates, or route high-uncertainty candidates to stronger verifiers. The final ranking score combines answer frequency, lightweight judge scores, optional process verifier scores, and strong verifier scores; unrouted candidates are not artificially penalized or boosted by missing strong verification signals. Three variants trace a cost-accuracy curve: CoBa-Routed-Light (2 candidates + lightweight verification), CoBa-Routed (max 4 candidates + 2 routed to strong verification), and CoBa-Routed-Strong (max 8 candidates + 4 routed to strong verification).a:T78a,方法核心是给标准 transformer 产生的 token-level latent h_t 再叠一层 higher-level abstract latent u_t，并在两个层级上同时施加自监督预测约束。具体地，模型先用主干 G_theta 从前缀 X_{1:t} 得到 lower-level latent h_t。然后用一个 sliding-window attention 模块，在最近 k 个 lower states 上做聚合，得到 higher-level latent u_t = SWA(h_{t-k+1:t})。这个设计不是任意加一层 pooling，而是显式把上层状态绑定到一个局部时间窗口，让它表示更粗粒度、跨多个 token 的抽象动态。训练时一共学四个映射：标准输出头 p_theta；lower dynamics p_psi，用于 NextLat 风格的一步 latent transition consistency；upper dynamics p_phi，用于在 abstract latent 上做 k 步前瞻预测；combined head p_rho，用 lower 与 upper latent 联合做 next-token prediction。总损失由五部分组成：标准 NTP loss、lower-level transition consistency loss、一个 KL 项、higher-level transition consistency loss、以及 combined NTP loss。论文里一个很细但重要的实现点是 stop-gradient 的放置：在 combined NTP 路径里，higher-level latent 由带 stop-grad 的 lower states 经过 SWA 得到。这样 combined head 的交叉熵只训练 SWA 和上层表示，不会反向改写 h_t。作者的理由很充分：如果不切断这条梯度，token-level 表示 h_t 会同时受到标准 NTP、lower transition consistency、以及经 SWA 回流的 combined-head 监督，形成重复计数和相互竞争的优化信号。这个处理说明作者不是把多头损失机械相加，而是在认真控制不同目标对表示空间的职责分工。另一个关键点是推理阶段只保留标准 NTP head，整个层级 apparatus 都是训练时辅助项，因此没有额外 inference overhead；如果愿意用速度换精度，也可以考虑 combined NTP，但当前实现默认不用。b:T5e6,HiLP starts from a standard transformer backbone that produces lower-level token latents h_t = G_theta(X_{1:t}). It then builds a higher-level abstract latent u_t by applying a sliding-window attention module over the most recent k lower-level states, u_t = SWA(h_{t-k+1:t}). This upper latent is meant to capture a coarser temporal abstraction over multiple tokens. The model jointly learns four parameterized components: the standard token output head p_theta, a lower-level dynamics model p_psi for NextLat-style transition consistency, an upper-level dynamics model p_phi that predicts k steps ahead in the abstract latent space, and a combined head p_rho that predicts the next token from both lower and upper latents. Training uses a weighted sum of five losses: standard NTP, lower-level latent transition consistency, a KL term, higher-level transition consistency, and a combined NTP loss. A subtle but important implementation detail is the stop-gradient placement. In the combined-NTP path, the upper latent is computed from stop-grad lower states before SWA. This prevents the combined-head cross-entropy from backpropagating into h_t through the SWA window. The authors explicitly argue that otherwise the lower token-level representation would receive overlapping and competing supervision from standard NTP, lower-level transition consistency, and the combined head. At inference time, only the standard NTP head is used, so the hierarchical apparatus acts purely as a training-time regularizer.c:T4f3,The experimental setup is reasonably substantial for a method paper: 1B-parameter models pretrained on 100B tokens using 8 NVIDIA B200 GPUs. Evaluation has two parts. First, zero-shot downstream performance is measured with LM Evaluation Harness on HumanEval and with DataComp for LLMs on symbolic and multi-step reasoning benchmarks. HiLP is reported to outperform the relevant baselines, including MTP and NextLat, especially on tasks that require longer-horizon reasoning or planning. Second, the paper evaluates speculative decoding on held-out code and Nemotron-Climbmix validation sets, reporting average accepted tokens per drafting step and draft–verifier argmax agreement for K=1..4. HiLP improves these metrics as well, suggesting that the learned latent dynamics are more rollout-stable and more useful for multi-token drafting. The most informative analysis is the latent prediction cross-entropy over rollout horizons up to 8 steps. HiLP preserves near-term prediction quality comparable to NextLat while reducing error at longer horizons, which directly supports the claim that hierarchical abstraction mitigates multi-step latent error accumulation. The excerpt does not include the full numeric tables, so exact gains cannot be quoted reliably here.d:T5a7,The main experiments use LLaDA-8B-Instruct. On GSM8K, pure decoding produces answer-only collapse on more than one third of problems with a 256-token canvas, and up to about 90% with a 512-token canvas. The final answer is typically committed in the first quarter of the denoising trajectory, while the median reasoning token is committed near the halfway point. A fixed 2×2 design crosses prompt type, reasoning requested versus answer-only, with decoder type, pure versus semi-AR. On 250 paired problems, the prompt–decoder interaction is about 35 percentage points with permutation p<1e-4. In the answer-only condition, the two decoders are statistically close. When CoT is requested, semi-AR gains about 31 points, while pure decoding gains only a small amount. The same pattern is replicated on Dream-7B and MATH-500; on MATH-500, collapse is smaller, but the order channel remains. Window restriction recovers most of the gap: all w≤64 improve pure decoding from 0.528 to roughly 0.82–0.85, close to semi-AR. A sliding window of 32 matches a block size of 32 within about one point, indicating that restricted reachability, rather than block structure itself, is the active mechanism. The paper also measures the cost: with full refinement, w=1 is best; at around eight committed tokens per step, the constraint can become harmful. In the useful regime, the gated decoder keeps near-AR accuracy with a reported 4× wall-clock speedup.e:T4c5,The controlled comparisons include Dense BLT, Hidden-only MoE, Hidden+Entropy MoE, and EntropyMoE, with matched data, updates, active parameters, and dense-copy initialization. The sparse setup uses 25 layers, 8 experts per layer, and Top-2 routing. The entropy-only router has about 400 effective routing parameters, compared with more than 4×10^5 parameters for hidden-state routing controls. EntropyMoE obtains the lowest held-out bits per byte, while the hidden-state MoE variants stay close to the dense baseline despite having the same expert pool and active capacity. The advantage over Hidden-only MoE is reproduced with a second continuation-training seed. Downstream accuracy is broadly similar; the clearest supported gain is on HellaSwag rather than a uniform task-level improvement. Byte-weighted accounting improves BPB over patch-count accounting in both tested seeds. Routing analysis shows clearer separation between low- and high-entropy patch assignments, but also concentration on a small subset of experts. Among sparse variants it has the best iteration time and valid-byte throughput, although sparse execution remains slower than Dense BLT because of dispatch, communication, and memory movement.f:T578,The method builds on the LapSum operator. Given scores r, a budget k, and a temperature parameter α<0, LapSum smooths the hard top-k threshold using the Laplace CDF and outputs probabilities p with the exact constraint ∑ p_i = k. The computational challenge is solving for the threshold b. Previous GPU implementations rely on bisection and rescan all n scores at each iteration.

Fast LapSum computes the whole threshold curve instead. After sorting, a stable prefix/suffix scan constructs the piecewise curve. The correct segment is found by an index lookup, and the threshold is obtained with a closed-form root calculation. The backward pass uses an analytical vector-Jacobian product with a single reduction, avoiding straight-through estimators or biased hard-mask surrogates.

For million-scale vectors, sorting itself becomes the bottleneck. The paper adds a probabilistic bracketing pipeline: a kernel-noised subsample localizes the likely threshold region, one streaming pass over the full vector keeps only the uncertain middle band, that band is sorted, and a verification step certifies the bracket before the exact LapSum solution is applied. This keeps expected work linear while still recovering the exact threshold and exact budget. Unlike DFTopK, which obtains linear-time behavior by relaxing the normalization constraint, Fast LapSum keeps the unrelaxed ∑ p_i = k constraint.10:T79f,The core system is AutoPrune. The central design choice is to avoid searching over full pruning programs from scratch. Instead, the method performs residual search around a strong base policy.

To make this possible, the authors introduce TPDSL, a token-pruning domain-specific language with 131 reusable atoms. These atoms cover four categories: budget control, token scoring, selection constraints, and token reassembly. The purpose is to compress the design space into structured operations that an LLM can manipulate reliably, rather than forcing it to rediscover the entire pruning algorithm in raw code.

The most important abstraction is the residual search-state representation. Each candidate consists of a strong base policy, a TPDSL-specified residual modification, and structural constraints that guarantee validity. At runtime, the base policy first selects tokens, and the residual policy is allowed to revise only a bounded quota of uncertain selections. This has several advantages: it preserves the reliable structure of existing human-designed policies, narrows the effective search space, and focuses the LLM on the local decisions that matter most. Because the residual rule is decoupled from runtime instantiation, the discovered policy can transfer across token budgets and model backbones.

Search is evaluator-in-the-loop. The LLM proposes TPDSL residuals, the system validates them against syntax and pruning constraints, then evaluates them on the target MLLM and feeds performance plus diagnostic signals back into the next iteration. This is closer to constrained policy search than to open-ended code generation.

Compared with prior pruning work, AutoPrune does not claim a single new importance metric. Instead, it treats existing heuristics as reusable structure and searches for better residual refinements on top of them. Compared with generic program synthesis, it wins by shrinking and structuring the search space.11:T511,The evaluation spans 14 multimodal benchmarks and 3 MLLM backbones. Two headline results stand out. First, under a 94.4% visual-token reduction ratio, AutoPrune preserves more than 99% of full-token performance on both LLaVA-1.5-7B and LLaVA-NeXT-7B. Second, in the 320-token efficiency setting on LLaVA-NeXT-7B, it reduces FLOPs by 9.9× and prefill latency by 6.4×. So the gains are not only in token count but also in actual inference cost.

The transfer experiments are especially important. The discovered policy transfers across token budgets without requiring a fresh search for every budget, and it also transfers across MLLM backbones. The paper further shows stable behavior across different LLM proposers, which supports the claim that the search-state representation is doing much of the heavy lifting. Another useful result is that AutoPrune consistently improves over multiple human-designed base policies, suggesting that residual search is not tied to one lucky baseline.

The ablations are where the design is best justified. Removing the residual formulation, replacing TPDSL with unconstrained full-program generation, or weakening structural constraints hurts both search efficiency and final pruning quality. Those results make the representation choice much more convincing.12:T52f,ReQuant is framed as fixed-grid discrete refinement. The quantization format is frozen (bit-width, scale, zero-point, grid layout, inference kernels). The only variables are the integer codes assigned to weights.

The objective follows reconstruction-based PTQ, with an explicit focus on activation mismatch: at inference, a layer sees activations produced by a quantized prefix. Similar to GPTAQ, it uses activation-aware reconstruction by collecting activations from (i) the full-precision model and (ii) the partially-quantized model to obtain \tilde{X}, then optimizes reconstruction under the more realistic activation distribution.

Optimization is backprop-free: instead of STE/gradient descent, it performs iterative discrete updates over integer codes (a coordinate-style local search). Updates are accepted only if they reduce the overall reconstruction loss, yielding monotonic loss decrease and finite termination (a finite number of accepted updates). The offline budget is controlled by the number of sweeps T, and the per-sweep cost is argued to be comparable to a GPTAQ pass.

A key design trade-off is deliberate: by freezing the grid and kernels, deployment complexity stays unchanged, but the search is constrained to the existing grid, so gains depend on the upstream choice of scales/grid being reasonable.13:T429,Its value for foundation-model research is diagnostic rather than merely competitive. First, it separates local perception from global spatial integration, which many existing video benchmarks do not. That matters for embodied pretraining, world modeling, and native multimodal training. Second, it shows that failure modes differ by model family: strong proprietary models improve sharply when the task is reduced to local perception, suggesting that their main weakness is cross-frame spatial integration; many open models do not improve reliably even then, indicating deficits in both perception and reasoning. Third, the paper goes beyond evaluation by introducing GST-Train and showing that targeted training data can substantially close the gap. Qwen3-VL-8B improves from 25.89 to 53.52 after fine-tuning, surpassing all zero-shot proprietary models in their setup. That is a useful signal for pretraining researchers: global spatial competence is not something to assume will emerge automatically from scale alone; data construction and task pressure matter.14:T5fc,The contribution is centered on benchmark and data design rather than a new VLM architecture. The pipeline is built on OmniGibson with BEHAVIOR-1K scenes. For each of 50 indoor environments, the system generates diverse egocentric camera trajectories and additional query viewpoints outside the trajectory. It records 3D coordinates of objects and viewpoints, projects them onto top-down maps, and automatically creates QA pairs. A key filtering rule removes any sample where the target object is visible from the query viewpoint, ensuring that object-localization questions cannot be solved from a single frame.

The task taxonomy is organized around three embodied questions: Where am I? Where is the target? What does the scene look like? These are instantiated as 12 subtasks covering self-localization, object localization, and scene-structure understanding. The top-down image is not cosmetic; it acts as an explicit probe of whether the model can map long-horizon egocentric evidence into a global scene representation.

A particularly strong part of the design is the controlled local variant. By making the target visible from the query viewpoint, the authors convert the task into one that can be handled largely by local perception. This creates a clean diagnostic split between failures caused by weak single-image understanding and failures caused by missing cross-view spatial integration. That makes GST-Bench more than a leaderboard asset; it becomes a tool for identifying what kind of spatial competence is absent.15:T584,The main evaluation covers 22 VLMs, including open-source, proprietary, and embodied-oriented models. The strongest zero-shot model, Gemini-3-Pro, reaches 42.68, far below the human score of 79.08. Open models are substantially weaker; the best reported open model, InternVL3.5-38B, stays around the low-30 range, and many models are close to random guessing. This gap is large enough that it is hard to dismiss as benchmark noise.

The local-variant analysis is one of the most informative results. Proprietary models can gain up to 39 points when the task is reduced to local perception, which strongly suggests that they already have decent local visual understanding but fail to maintain and use a global spatial state across frames. Open models, in contrast, do not improve consistently even in the local setting, pointing to a compound bottleneck involving both perception and reasoning.

The training-data experiment is equally important. Using the same generation pipeline, the authors build GST-Train from BEHAVIOR-1K, ArtVIP, HyperSim, and internal simulation scenes. Fine-tuning Qwen3-VL-8B on GST-Train raises its GST-Bench score from 25.89 to 53.52, a gain of 27.63 points, and pushes it above all proprietary models under zero-shot evaluation. That result suggests the benchmark is learnable with targeted supervision and that synthetic spatial data can materially improve global spatial reasoning.16:T471,Three components are described in the provided text:
1) SearchAuditBench: 1,243 failed trajectories collected from 8 open-weight models run under a unified scaffold (two tools: search, visit) on 5 deep-search benchmarks. Each trajectory is expert-annotated with (i) the critical error step plus a tolerance span, (ii) a 6-way search-specific root-cause taxonomy, and (iii) a reference repair directive paired with atomic grading rubrics. Only “trace-auditable” failures are kept—annotators must be able to justify the critical error using evidence inside the frozen trace.
2) Benchmarking auditors: evaluate LLMs on localization, attribution, and repair, highlighting challenges from scale/noise, lack of verifiable structure, and silent error propagation.
3) SearchAuditor method: run three complementary audit branches in parallel, adjudicate their reports over a compact evidence-grounded view of the trajectory, then synthesize process-level repair directives.

The excerpt does not specify what the three branches are or how the compact view is constructed; those are method-critical details that require the missing sections.17:T40c,Visual tool-use is formulated as a structural causal graph that separates three paths to the final answer: the direct path from raw input, observation-mediated paths (where visual content of tool outputs genuinely affects the answer), and action-induced shortcuts (where only the text of tool calls influences the answer). Three intervention protocols with increasing granularity are designed: 1) Policy-level: compare overall performance between tool-use and direct inference; 2) Trajectory-level: corrupt all returned observations at runtime and measure answer changes; 3) Step-level: replace a single observation with a counterfactual crop and compute Visual Evidence Gain (corresponding to Natural Indirect Effect) to judge whether the visual content truly alters answer preference. Based on intervention results, each rollout is categorized into four groups: Calibrated, Calling Without Looking (CWL), Looking Without Planning (LWP), and No-call; policy-level accuracy improvements are then decomposed into per-group contributions.18:T83e,方法核心是把 AIVAT 和 confidence sequences 结合成 AV-AIVAT。AIVAT 原本用于不完全信息博弈中的方差缩减，它利用 value function 对某些决策节点构造条件均值为零的校正项，从而在不引入偏差的前提下降低 payoff 方差。问题在于，经典 AIVAT 默认 value function 是冻结的，而实际评测中人们往往希望边评边学，让 value model 越来越准。论文提出的关键接口是 predictable / pre-action conditional-kernel interface：在每个 correction node，评测器必须在动作发生前就知道该动作在当下信息条件下的 conditional kernel，而且是否启用该 correction 也必须在动作前就固定。与此同时，时刻 t 的 value function 只能使用 1 到 t-1 局的数据，不能用当前局。这样可以保证当前样本不会“选择自己的校正项”，从而保住 conditional mean-zero 的性质。

在这个接口之上，论文给了两类 confidence sequence。第一类是 EB-CS，强调 exact finite-sample validity，但要求事先给出一个与数据无关、几乎处处成立的 corrected payoff 上界 B。作者在 Leduc 上推导并穷举验证了这样的 bound。第二类是 AsympCS，放宽为渐近有效，需要满足极限方差条件，实际中更高效。论文还给出一个理论结果：在 variance-adaptive width 和 sublinear variance-regret 条件下，past-only online learner 的停止效率在渐近上不会比 deterministic oracle-width benchmark 慢。直观上，这说明在线学 value model 只要不偷看当前局，就可以逐步逼近“知道最好 value function”的评测效率。

另一个很有价值的分析是 width floor。作者指出，方差缩减并不总会等比例转化为更早停止，因为在 bet-capped EB-CS 下，声明的 payoff bound 会引入一个确定性的宽度下界 4B log(2/α)/t。一旦这个 floor 开始主导，继续降低方差也不会明显缩短停止时间。论文把这件事整理成三阶段的 order-level design guide，这比单纯报告“方差降了多少倍”更有解释力。19:T866,The method combines AIVAT with confidence sequences to form AV-AIVAT. AIVAT is a variance-reduction estimator for imperfect-information games. It uses a value function to construct conditional mean-zero corrections at selected decision points, reducing payoff variance without introducing bias. Classical AIVAT assumes a frozen value function, but in practice one would like the value model to improve during evaluation. The key contribution is a predictable, pre-action conditional-kernel interface. At each correction node, the evaluator must know the action’s conditional kernel given the information available immediately before the action, and the decision to enable the correction must also be fixed before the action is observed. In addition, the value function at time t may use only games 1 through t-1, never game t itself. These conditions prevent the current sample from choosing its own correction and preserve the conditional mean-zero property.

On top of this interface, the paper develops two confidence-sequence options. EB-CS provides exact finite-sample validity, but it requires a data-independent almost-sure bound on the corrected payoff. The authors derive and exhaustively verify such a bound in Leduc. AsympCS instead relies on asymptotic variance conditions and is more efficient in practice. The theory also includes a no-asymptotic-delay style result: under variance-adaptive widths and sublinear variance regret, a past-only online value learner asymptotically matches a deterministic oracle-width benchmark. Intuitively, online value learning can approach oracle stopping efficiency as long as it does not peek at the current game.

A particularly useful part of the paper is the width-floor analysis. Variance reduction does not automatically translate into proportionally earlier stopping. In the bet-capped EB-CS setting, the declared payoff bound induces a deterministic lower bound on interval width, 4B log(2/alpha) / t. Once this floor dominates, further variance reduction has little effect on stopping time. The resulting three-regime design guide is more informative than reporting variance reduction alone.1a:T688,The main empirical study uses a heads-up no-limit hold’em corpus with 71,439 paired hands across 15 LLM-agent configurations. AIVAT reduces variance by a median factor of 54 relative to raw per-hand payoffs. The more important metric is stopping time. At alpha = 0.05 and a target half-width of one big blind, paired replay shows a median raw-to-AIVAT stopping-time ratio of 74 under AsympCS. In practical terms, the same evaluation precision can be reached with roughly one seventy-fourth as many hands. Under the descriptive EB-CS execution, the ratio is only 1.37. The paper explains this large gap through the width floor: once the deterministic bound-driven floor dominates, lower variance no longer buys much earlier stopping.

For online learning, the authors test past-only value updates under two controlled profile-mismatch settings. The online learner recovers 77% to 79% of the frozen-to-oracle variance gap, which supports the claim that online improvement is possible without sacrificing validity.

The rolling-leaderboard simulation is also important. Under the null, continuously monitoring an ordinary fixed-sample interval and stopping at the first favorable look yields a 61% false-positive rate. Recomputing a confidence sequence at that same stopping time overturns essentially all such claims. When AsympCS continues under its own stopping rule, it detects injected effects of 0.1 and 0.2 big blinds in 81% and 100% of entries by 5,000 hands. The paper also specifies what must be released for reproducibility: corrected outcomes up to the stopping time, the stopping rule, the target, the confidence level, the declared bound, and the CS settings.1b:T4f4,For a quick read, focus on three things. First, the motivation for why ordinary optional stopping fails and why AIVAT and confidence sequences solve complementary parts of the problem. Second, the definition of the pre-action conditional-kernel interface, which is the central validity condition of the paper. Third, the width-floor analysis and the HUNL results, because they explain when variance reduction does and does not convert into cheaper evaluation.

A good reading order is: problem setup and motivation, the AV-AIVAT interface, the distinction between EB-CS and AsympCS, the HUNL experiments, and the rolling-leaderboard simulation. The proof details can be skimmed at first, but it is worth understanding what Proposition 1, Proposition 2, and Theorem 2 each guarantee. Around 40 to 60 minutes should be enough to extract the main ideas and practical implications.

Two caveats are worth keeping in mind. The method assumes access to the action conditional kernel, which may be hard to obtain in some real agent environments. And AsympCS is more efficient but does not provide the same exact finite-sample certificate as EB-CS. Which variant is appropriate depends on whether strict certification or sample efficiency matters more in the target benchmark.1c:T55a,The core design is to hold the single-shot solver fixed and vary one memory choice at a time. The paper decomposes future value into three settings. First, exact-query replay: if the same question returns, can the system cheaply replay the stored verified SQL? Second, cross-question retention: remove a question’s own card from the bank and test whether the remaining cards still help answer it, isolating reuse across questions rather than direct memorization. Third, held-out same-database transfer: evaluate whether memory helps new questions from the same database. To make transfer interpretable, the authors define CR, the crystallization ratio, as held-out transfer lift divided by the on-demand repair headroom on those same held-out questions. This measures how much of the gain that would otherwise require fresh repair can be recovered from memory at first attempt. In the main instantiation, execution-guided repair generates episodes, which are then stored in a database-scoped verified card bank. The paper runs controlled interventions around three questions: where future value appears, what information actually carries transfer, and which choices in collection, verification, formatting, and retrieval materially affect outcomes. An important detail is that the card writer never sees gold SQL, which avoids leaking supervision into the memory bank.1d:T52f,The main experiments are on BIRD, with three paired seeds and analysis that accounts for variation across both databases and questions. The headline result is that storing verified corrected queries in a database-scoped bank improves held-out first-attempt accuracy by 4.34 percentage points. This gain corresponds to 44.4% of the on-demand repair headroom on the same held-out questions. In practical terms, nearly half of the benefit that would otherwise require extra repair computation can be recovered from previously accumulated memory. The paper also reports clear value in replay and retention settings, indicating that the bank is not only memorizing exact solved questions but also carrying some reusable database-specific knowledge. The mechanism studies are especially informative. Transfer is driven mainly by exposure to the target database: local cards reveal useful identifiers, values, joins, and query structures, and they still help even when question-SQL pairings are shuffled. That suggests the transferable signal is more about database-local patterns and query priors than exact aligned demonstrations. Reliable verification and broad retrieval coverage produce clear gains, while richer card formats, more elaborate retrievers, and repair-specific content do not show statistically supported advantages.1e:T59d,The paper has a structural part and an empirical part. Structurally, for an odd-numbered unweighted majority panel with k judges and a single replacement ballot, the panel prediction can change if and only if the original vote margin is one, m = |2s-k| = 1. This is a deterministic arithmetic fact about majority voting, not a statistical claim. It identifies the exact affected set for single-ballot substitution.

Empirically, the authors build multi-model judge panels for code-generation evaluation and introduce a verification signal from a different evidence source. They compare several replacement rules, including random replacement, fixed replacement, and replacing a vote on the majority side. They then report two diagnostics on the same data: the same effective-vote statistic used in prior work, and margin-stratified accuracy/error analyses. The key result is that these diagnostics answer different questions. Aggregate dependence can remain unchanged while conditional gains on pivotal examples are large and reliable.

The mechanism is simple. Most examples are structurally unaffected by single-ballot substitution, so averaging over the full population dilutes any verifier benefit. If the verifier is especially useful where the panel is near the decision boundary, the gain is real but localized. The paper also includes an exploratory analysis linking part of the pivotal-region error pattern to panel composition.1f:T552,The experiments cover three code benchmarks and four panel sizes, k in {3, 5, 7, 9}. The main setting uses seven LLM judges plus one verification signal. At larger scale, replacing one judge with the verifier changes the effective-vote count by about -0.04 with a 95% confidence interval of [-0.10, +0.02], so the aggregate metric shows no distinguishable improvement. Yet panel accuracy still increases.

The margin-stratified analysis explains why. For non-pivotal cases, such as at least 5–2 agreement in a 7-judge panel, single-ballot substitution cannot alter the majority outcome, so the accuracy gain is exactly 0. For 4–3 cases, the same verification signal improves accuracy by more than 10 percentage points. In the main setting, 199 of 569 panel errors occur in the pivotal region. So pivotal cases do not contain all errors, but they have higher error rates, and every decision change induced by single-ballot substitution is confined there.

The paper also evaluates margin gating. Once a substitution rule is fixed, verifier calls outside the pivotal region can be skipped without changing predictions. In their experiments, this reduces verifier-call frequency to 12%–27% while preserving the same outputs as universal substitution. Table 7 further shows that the replacement rule itself materially affects accuracy; gating only saves calls.20:T4a5,Proposes PaDoc, a layout-grounded parallel document parser built on a region-sufficiency assumption: conditioned on the full page image and a region's layout specification, content generation for that region is independent of content from other regions. The joint parsing distribution is factorized into a serial layout prediction stream, and parallel content generation branches spawned immediately after each layout is predicted, reducing logical decoding depth from the sum of all region content lengths to the longest single layout-content path. During training, an ancestor attention mask restricts each content branch to attend only to the shared page image and historical layout prefix, with no access to sibling region content. This is implemented with packed variable-length sequences, requiring no auxiliary prediction heads or extra losses, and retaining the standard next-token prediction objective. During inference, PaDoc runs on vLLM with global shared KV cache for the page image and layout prefix, decoding each region content branch as a concurrent request. The model is initialized from Qwen3-VL-2B, adapted via continual pre-training followed by supervised fine-tuning.21:T648,The work has two main parts. First, it establishes and explains the mismatch. Under fixed top-k input settings, the authors evaluate three multimodal DLMs on five visual QA benchmarks using the same VisRAG-ranked candidate pools, prompts, decoding budgets, and answer scorer. They show that Recall@k increases with k, while macro-average answer accuracy drops. They then isolate the cause and find that semantic conflict, not merely the presence of multiple images, is the main driver of degradation. The theoretical analysis focuses on the first unresolved denoising state. When a correct page and a conflicting page each support a different coherent answer, the correct latent structure requires multiple answer positions to share one source choice. A position-factorized proposal breaks this dependence and allocates probability mass to cross-source answers; the unsupported mass grows as the conflict spans more answer positions. Second, the paper proposes ECF, a training-free evidence admission framework. It combines multi-granularity evidence construction, where both full pages and layout-derived regions are indexed, with generator-side candidate filtering. For each additional candidate ci, ECF compares the target DLM’s first-step answer-block entropy under {c1, ci} versus {c1, blank(ci)}. This yields a conditional confidence gain that controls for the structural effect of adding another visual slot and measures whether the candidate content itself reduces competing-source risk. A rank-prior selection rule then decides whether to expand beyond the top-1 page and which candidate to admit.22:T4d4,The evaluation spans three multimodal DLMs and five visual QA benchmarks. The main empirical findings come in three layers. First, the phenomenon itself: increasing top-k from 1 to 2 or 3 consistently raises answer-page recall, but lowers macro-average answer accuracy across 15 model-dataset combinations. Second, the mechanism: conflicting pages produce the largest accuracy drop among the tested evidence conditions. The same interference is already visible in the first-step answer-block distribution. On naturally retrieved ChartQA cases, the conflicting-page condition causes the largest drop in first-step answer-block accuracy and the largest Jensen-Shannon divergence from the correct-only distribution, and this aligns with the largest final-answer degradation. Third, the method result: across five benchmarks and three DLMs, ECF improves over the strongest fixed top-k input by 2.62 percentage points on average. With LLaDA2.0-Uni, it also beats the strongest training-free baseline by 2.37 points on average. The paper further reports that ECF remains more stable as the candidate pool grows, which supports the claim that it decouples candidate-pool size from final context size rather than merely tuning a better fixed k.23:T7d7,KVAE is a family of encoder-decoder tokenizers for multimodal generative modeling. The visual side includes two causal video tokenizers: KVAE-3D with 4x8x8 compression and 16 latent channels, and KVAE-3D with 4x16x16 compression and 64 channels. The image tokenizer, KVAE-2D, uses 8x spatial compression with 32 channels. The audio tokenizer is continuous, full-band 48 kHz, with a 50 Hz latent stream and 64 channels.

The most interesting design choices appear in the video tokenizer. First, the backbone is attention-free and built around Conv3D blocks. The motivation is efficiency and length generalization: convolutional tokenizers are cheaper to train and can process arbitrarily long sequences at inference through caching, which is useful for long videos and causal generation settings. Second, the tokenizer is causal, implemented with asymmetric temporal padding, so it can support both images and videos without leaking future frames. Third, temporal and spatial downsampling/upsampling are applied sequentially rather than collapsed into a single operation. The paper contrasts this with approaches that patchify aggressively in early layers; KVAE-4x16x16 delays patching to the downsampling blocks instead. Fourth, the models use spatial RMSNorm, which allows segment-size adjustment at inference. Fifth, the encoder and decoder are intentionally asymmetric, especially in the higher-compression model where the decoder is made substantially wider. This reflects a clear hypothesis: under strong compression, it is better to keep the encoder stable and efficient while giving the decoder more capacity to reconstruct detail.

Another important point is that the paper treats diffusability as a primary tokenizer property. Reconstruction metrics alone are not enough. A tokenizer can reconstruct well yet still produce a latent space that is awkward for diffusion training. The later sections on diffusability evaluation and design ablations are therefore central to the paper’s contribution.24:T6c7,The reported evaluation spans reconstruction, downstream generation quality, diffusability analysis, and architecture ablations. On the visual side, KVAE-3D is compared against open-source video tokenizers on both reconstruction and generation, using metrics such as PSNR, LPIPS, and Fréchet-style generation scores. KVAE-2D is evaluated similarly for images. On the audio side, the paper reports objective metrics such as PESQ and emphasizes the practical operating point of full-band 48 kHz audio with a 50 Hz latent stream and 64 channels.

The abstract claims that KVAE matches or exceeds open-source alternatives on both reconstruction and generation. However, the provided full-text excerpt does not include the complete result tables, so it is not possible to restate all dataset-specific numbers reliably from the available material alone. Even so, the experimental structure already conveys several useful points. The tokenizer is evaluated not only as an autoencoder but also as part of the full generation pipeline, which is the right criterion. The video tokenizer is designed for long-sequence and causal settings, so the relevant comparison is not just short-clip reconstruction. And the paper explicitly includes diffusability evaluation and design ablations, which suggests the authors are trying to explain why the latent space is easier to model, not merely that it reconstructs better.

When reading the full paper, the most important quantitative checks are likely to be: the trade-off between the 4x16x16(64ch) and 4x8x8(16ch) variants in token cost versus generation quality; the contribution of the wider decoder; and whether the attention-free design preserves enough semantic structure under high compression.25:T514,Read it in four passes: problem framing, architecture, end-to-end validation, and ablations. Start with the introduction to see how the authors position tokenizers as part of the generative model rather than a detachable front-end. Then focus on the video-tokenizer method section. The key design decisions are the attention-free Conv3D backbone, causal setup, spatial RMSNorm, sequential temporal/spatial downsampling, and encoder-decoder asymmetry. The useful question to keep in mind is why these choices are preferable to heavier spatiotemporal attention or more aggressive early patchification.

After that, inspect the reconstruction and generation tables together. Do not read PSNR or LPIPS in isolation; check whether a tokenizer that reconstructs well also improves downstream generation. Then read the diffusability section and the ablations carefully. That is where the paper either becomes a set of engineering choices or turns into a more general tokenizer-design lesson. If your interest is unified multimodal pretraining, the audio section is worth reading as well because it extends similar design principles to high-rate continuous audio. A first pass should take around 40 minutes, and a reproduction-oriented read will require careful attention to the method and ablation sections.26:T521,From the available full-text excerpt (intro only), the approach is organized around three coupled issues and corresponding design choices:
1) Unified formulation: treat generation, reference-based synthesis, temporal extension, and editing—now extended to joint audio-video—as one conditional denoising family with varying condition/target configurations.
2) Explicit token roles: instead of implicit concatenation, attach role information to tokens so the model is told which segments are reconstruction targets, which are to be preserved, and which are guidance-only references.
3) Conditioning duality: implement both a semantic pathway (instruction following, reasoning over text + visual evidence) and a latent pathway (high-fidelity preservation of identity/texture/layout/motion). In audio-video, this must also handle modality absence and asymmetric roles per modality.
4) Data consistency & balance: emphasize truly aligned audio-video pairs and a task mixture that does not let data-rich objectives dominate.

However, the excerpt stops before the method section; details such as the exact role representation, backbone topology (single-stream vs dual-tower), fusion mechanism, loss weighting, and sampling of condition-output configurations cannot be stated faithfully without the missing sections.27:T5eb,Two pillars.
(1) MIST-1000 benchmark: each item is instantiated into four matched variants where the question, answer space/type, gold answer, and a plausible wrong answer are held fixed; only the surrounding context changes: clean (no added context), misleading (context nudges toward the plausible wrong answer), correct-context (context supports the gold), and irrelevant (natural related text that does not contain the answer). The dataset contains 1,000 items (800 adapted from existing QA/math/reasoning benchmarks, 200 newly written longer scenarios) with a two-pass human QA process to control ambiguity, leakage, and plausibility.
(2) Metrics + training:
- SC2W (signal-induced correct-to-wrong): computed on the subset of items the model answers correctly in the clean condition, measuring how often misleading context flips them to wrong—separating susceptibility from base competence.
- SCOPE (Selective Context Preference Optimization): a signal-counterfactual preference framework. It mines the critical failures from a base model: cases where the model is correct in clean but wrong under misleading context. For each, it forms a preference pair (truth-consistent response preferred over signal-following response). The key is not changing DPO’s objective, but balancing the training mixture: reuse the same preference pair across all four context conditions and weight misleading pairs equally against clean/correct/irrelevant controls, preventing the “learn to ignore all context” shortcut.28:T439,The proposed method is Ranking-based Reward Construction. It uses a generative reward model only for relative preference ranking, then derives scalar rewards from the resulting order. The first mechanism is self-competitive ranking: for a given prompt, multiple responses sampled from the current policy are compared against each other, and their relative order is mapped into rewards. This aligns naturally with group-relative policy optimization. The second mechanism is anchor-guided ranking: a small set of anchor responses serves as reference points, and candidate responses are compared against these anchors. This reduces the reward-model call pattern for larger candidate sets from a sorting-style O(m log m) regime to O(m n), where m is the number of sampled responses and n is the number of anchors. When n is small, this is more scalable. Unlike GenRM-style preference-token probabilities, RRC does not rely on a single token probability. Unlike approaches that suppress reasoning and emit only labels, it can preserve the generative judge’s textual reasoning process.29:T4a6,HERALD treats a reward as a program and subjects it to structured counterfactual unit tests on the same question rather than evaluating a trained policy by aggregate score. First, it constructs minimal same-question trajectory interventions: same-final deletes all search actions while preserving the answer and citations; fake-ID replaces a citation with a nonexistent corpus ID; adaptive laundering replaces it with a real passage that the agent never retrieved. Second, it draws a strict information boundary: attack generation is label-free and accesses only the question, logged trajectory, and corpus, never gold answers or support annotations; if reward computation uses annotated signals, that selection is reported as an oracle upper bound rather than a candidate-visible attack. Third, it enumerates the full 2^3 lattice over three detectors—retrieval-use, membership in retrieved evidence, and fake IDs—and reports paired reward margins to identify an inclusion-minimal repair instead of claiming victory from a large detector bundle. The key detector L is an exact membership check that fires when a cited passage exists in the corpus but was absent from retrieved evidence.2a:T465,The audit is run on four Qwen3-8B trajectory pools from HotpotQA, 2WikiMultiHopQA, and MuSiQue, covering 3,829 isolated trajectories. The base reward R0 already rejects same-final search deletion and fake citation IDs, but a label-free adaptive citation-laundering attack succeeds: replacing a citation with a real but unretrieved corpus passage can still achieve high reward. The full 2^3 ablation shows that strengthening L alone is the observed minimal repair on these pools, while the other two exact checks are redundant. With L added, R[L] reports zero empirical attack success rate at roughly 0.50% one-sided cost on the relevant metric, while a broader strict reward remains attackable because its oracle support-ID penalty can be removed or avoided. A matched 5M-token Search-GRPO comparison shows that only 18 of 58,368 training trajectories trigger the detector, so the training signal is extremely sparse; on 256 paired questions per benchmark, R[L] improves targeted attackability and some evidence-quality measures but does not guarantee lower natural violation incidence or uniform exact-match non-inferiority.2b:T472,The method follows the OPD / OPD2 line. In OPD, the student first samples a rollout from its current policy. That sampled trajectory is then scored token by token by the teacher, and the student is updated to reduce the discrepancy on the sampled tokens. Compared with sequence-level RL, this gives denser token-level supervision while preserving the on-policy nature. OPD2 changes the reward definition: instead of using log p_teacher minus log p_student, it uses log p_teacher minus log p_teacher_base. The motivation is to isolate capabilities acquired during post-training, especially reasoning, while suppressing generic language preferences and stylistic biases already present in the teacher’s base model. The paper also keeps the two stabilizers introduced in prior OPD2 work: reward centering to form an advantage-like signal, and a conditioning criterion based on the original OPD signal to avoid pathological convergence. Empirically, the study builds a 100K multilingual math training set in English, Korean, and Japanese, and compares multilingual OPD and OPD2 as well as English-only OPD on Qwen3 instruction-tuned models.2c:T6a0,The method edits a single weight matrix W at a time, either the LM head or the down-projection in an MLP block. The model performs one forward pass on forget data and one on retain data, collects the input activations to W, and stacks them as key matrices. Keys are extracted only at positions where the next token should be forgotten or preserved, so the optimization is token-level rather than sample-level.

The objective is a ridge-regularized least-squares problem over an additive update ΔW. One term pushes the edited matrix toward a predefined forgetting target on forget keys. Another term constrains outputs on retain keys to stay close to the original model. Because the objective is quadratic and convex in ΔW, the optimum has a closed-form solution. This is the main departure from methods such as gradient ascent, RKLD, NPO, or preference-style unlearning, which search for a compromise through SGD.

The paper uses two target designs. Token suppression directly lowers the model’s tendency to emit the memorized answer token or related logits. Representation corruption instead perturbs intermediate representations so downstream layers cannot easily reconstruct the deleted content. The authors also introduce a logit-lens-based layer selection strategy: they inspect which intermediate layers already make the target content linearly decodable through the LM head, then edit only those high-impact layers. This avoids broad model changes. A notable extra is exact influence auditing. Because the update is analytic, the contribution of an individual deletion request to the final parameter edit can be decomposed exactly, which is much harder with iterative gradient methods.2d:T66d,The paper evaluates forgetting-utility trade-offs on five benchmarks and uses gold retraining as a reference point. Three findings matter most. First, efficiency: GROM needs only forward-pass statistics for forget and retain sets plus a closed-form solve per edited layer, leading to second-level execution and up to two orders of magnitude speedup over gradient-based baselines. Second, effectiveness: on Pareto trade-offs, it is typically better than or competitive with GA, GradDiff, RMU, RKLD, NPO, and SimNPO, meaning stronger forgetting at matched utility or better utility at matched forgetting. Third, robustness: under the recent low-bit quantization recovery attack, a strong gradient-based baseline regains a substantial amount of supposedly erased content, while GROM remains close to the retrained model in forgetfulness.

The most informative ablations are not just average scores but the structure of the edit: which layers are edited, whether the LM head or MLP down-projection is modified, token suppression versus representation corruption, the ridge coefficient, the size of the retain set, and interference across multiple deletion requests. The broader experimental message is that local linear edits can handle a meaningful fraction of memory-style unlearning when layer selection is accurate and retain constraints are strong, but distributed multi-layer knowledge remains a harder case. The provided full-text excerpt does not include all numeric tables, so exact benchmark-by-benchmark scores cannot be reproduced here, but the speedup scale, Pareto behavior, and quantization robustness are the key empirical takeaways.2e:T782,实验最有信息量的部分有三块。第一块是校准本身是否必要。论文指出，进入 contrastive calibration 的候选任务都已经通过结构验证和 self-solving，但初始时只有 19% 满足“强通过、弱失败”的目标关系；经过多轮 revision 和 re-probing，累计接受率提升到 96%。这说明 executable validity 与 solver-relative learnability 之间有明显缺口，而且这个缺口可以通过行为驱动的重写显著缩小。

第二块是不同数据构造策略对下游 agent 训练的影响。作者构建了 5,431 个校准后的 terminal tasks，并比较了几种训练数据来源：仅 authoring+validation、单 solver 反馈、多 solver calibration、contrastive calibration。Terminal-Bench 2.0 上，authoring+validation 只有 22.47%，单 solver 反馈 24.34%，multi-solver calibration 提升到 29.21%，contrastive calibration 31.09%。这里的提升不是边际噪声，而是相对清晰地说明：只知道任务“能做”不够，知道“谁能做、谁做不好”更有训练价值。

第三块是用完整校准数据训练模型后的结果。CalibForge-30B-A3B 和 CalibForge-35B-A3B 在 Terminal-Bench 2.0 上分别达到 32.58% 和 47.57%，比最强 baseline 高 6.36 和 6.75 个百分点。更关键的是泛化：35B 模型在 SWE-bench Pro 上 44.32%，在 Doc2Repo 上 48.77%，都是同 backbone 下所比较训练数据源里最好的结果。这个结果支持一个判断：校准得到的不是只对合成任务有效的“刷榜数据”，而是更接近真实软件工程交互中有效 supervision 的数据分布。

从消融角度，最值得注意的是 single-solver feedback 与 multi/contrastive calibration 的差距。它说明单一 solver 的成功或失败信号太粗，容易把任务推向该 solver 的局部偏好，而多 solver 分歧或强弱对比能提供更稳定的难度定位信号。2f:T8e4,CalibForge starts from a clue and builds a terminal task automatically, but the real novelty is the adversarial solver calibration stage after initial authoring. The pipeline has four main parts. First, an authoring agent works in a draft sandbox with web search, shell execution, and file editing. It researches concrete engineering issues from documentation, GitHub repositories and issues, and Stack Overflow, looking for reproducible problems such as dependency conflicts, version-specific bugs, configuration pitfalls, and edge cases. Second, it jointly writes the task instruction, execution environment, and verification tests, then performs structural validation and self-solving so the candidate is executable, checkable, and solvable by the system itself.

Third, the candidate enters calibration. The paper defines two retention criteria. Multi-solver calibration seeks disagreement in a heterogeneous solver pool: at least one solver must pass and at least one must fail. Contrastive calibration is stricter: a designated stronger solver must pass while a designated weaker solver must fail. In both cases, learnability is defined relationally, not by an absolute difficulty score. Fourth, if the criterion is not met, the authoring agent revises the task and revalidates it before another probing round. Importantly, revision is not limited to wording tweaks. The agent may revisit external research, change the technical direction, rewrite the environment, modify tests, or alter constraints.

Two design choices matter. First, the feedback includes not only verified pass/fail outcomes but also full trajectories. That gives the authoring agent actionable evidence about why a task failed or succeeded, which can be used to reduce noise, sharpen constraints, or introduce steps that better separate strong and weak solvers. Second, the loop is adversarial but constrained: retention always requires at least one real successful solve, so the system does not drift toward pathological or unsolvable tasks. Compared with one-shot authoring plus validation, or with single-solver scoring, this adds a behavior-driven revision loop. That is the key difference, because learnability is treated as something that can be actively shaped rather than passively measured.30:T76d,The most informative results come in three layers. First, calibration is clearly necessary. All candidates entering contrastive calibration have already passed structural validation and self-solving, yet only 19% initially satisfy the target strong-pass/weak-fail relation. After iterative revision and re-probing, cumulative acceptance rises to 96%. This shows that executable validity and solver-relative learnability are far from equivalent, and that the gap can be closed by behavior-guided rewriting.

Second, the paper compares training data produced by different construction strategies. On Terminal-Bench 2.0, authoring plus validation alone yields 22.47% accuracy. Adding single-solver feedback gives 24.34%. Multi-solver calibration reaches 29.21%, and contrastive calibration reaches 31.09%. The gain is large enough to support a clear interpretation: knowing that a task is solvable is not enough; knowing which solvers succeed and which fail provides substantially better supervision.

Third, the authors train on the full set of 5,431 calibrated tasks. CalibForge-30B-A3B and CalibForge-35B-A3B obtain 32.58% and 47.57% on Terminal-Bench 2.0, outperforming the strongest baselines by 6.36 and 6.75 points. The transfer results matter even more. The 35B model reaches 44.32% on SWE-bench Pro and 48.77% on Doc2Repo, the best among the compared training-data sources for the same backbone. That suggests the calibrated tasks are not merely overfit synthetic exercises; they appear to induce supervision that transfers to broader software-engineering settings.

The ablation that stands out is the gap between single-solver feedback and multi-solver or contrastive calibration. A single solver provides a coarse signal and can bias task construction toward that solver’s idiosyncrasies. Solver disagreement and strong-vs-weak contrast provide a more stable handle on difficulty placement.31:T5ce,If time is limited, start with the Introduction sections that define the solver-relative learnable zone, then read the methodology around Figure 2 and Algorithm 1 to understand the control flow of the author–solver loop. After that, focus on the two calibration criteria, multi-solver and contrastive. Those sections contain the real contribution. Without them, the system would be just another task-generation pipeline.

In the experiments, prioritize three result blocks. First, the fraction of initially authored tasks that already satisfy the contrastive relation versus the cumulative acceptance after revision rounds. Second, the comparison among authoring-only, single-solver feedback, multi-solver calibration, and contrastive calibration on Terminal-Bench 2.0. Third, the final 30B and 35B training results and their transfer to SWE-bench Pro and Doc2Repo. Those are the results that tell you whether the method changes supervision quality rather than just producing more tasks.

Three deeper questions are worth following while reading. How exactly are trajectories converted into revision actions? How sensitive is the learnable zone to the composition of the solver pool? And can this idea generalize beyond terminal tasks to code completion, tool-use, web agents, or even difficulty-aware sampling in broader pretraining settings? For reproduction, it would be sensible to test the value of multi-solver disagreement filtering before rebuilding the full authoring system.32:T616,For each task instance, Gated-BEPO samples a group of K rollouts. Environment observations are converted into hashable state keys, and identical observed states are merged into an empirical graph. Edges correspond to rollout records: state, action, reward, and next state. Instead of assigning uniform trajectory credit, the method runs a mean-backup Bellman fixed-point evaluation on this empirical graph to estimate node values under the current rollout policy. It then computes fixed-point temporal-difference residuals and propagates them along each trajectory using GAE to obtain step-level advantages.

The central addition is a graph-confidence gate. If a state has only one observed successor, the graph provides no empirical basis for comparing alternative transitions, so the Bellman step credit is suppressed and the update relies mainly on group-relative outcome advantage. If a state has multiple observed successors, the local graph contains comparative evidence, so the Bellman credit is activated and given more weight, while the outcome signal is still retained as a stabilizer.

This differs from GRPO, which uses only trajectory-level credit; from GiGPO and HGPO, which rely on state or history matching to form local groups; from GAGPO, which propagates a grouped return proxy with TD/GAE-style estimates; and from GraphGPO, which uses graph distance to successful terminal states and can be sensitive to perceptual aliasing. Gated-BEPO instead evaluates the empirical rollout graph and gates its contribution based on observed branching.33:T451,DiDPO extends group-relative advantage from the trajectory level into the internal structure of a code diff.

Using a CodeAct-like interaction loop, each action yields an edit (add/del/none) and the environment provides an outcome reward (e.g., tests pass/fail). Instead of treating the whole response/diff as one credit carrier, DiDPO:
1) Aligns diffs across multiple rollouts for the same task by finding highly similar sub-diffs that recur across trajectories.
2) Uses these recurring sub-diffs as anchors to induce sub-diff boundaries within larger diffs, aiming to isolate functional regions.
3) Groups aligned sub-diffs and computes a local group-relative advantage (diff-level advantage), then combines it with the trajectory-level advantage and assigns it back to the tokens responsible for generating those sub-diffs.

To avoid anchors that are too broad (mixing functions) or too narrow (hard to aggregate), it introduces a groupability score to select anchors that balance semantic scope and group mass. The method remains critic-free and claims no additional rollouts beyond the sampled group.34:T41a,TRIAL keeps the GRPO rollout-level advantage and clipping rule unchanged, then adds a training-only hindsight objective. For each decision turn, it builds an outcome view that exposes the realized local consequence of that decision. The same generated response is scored under the ordinary interaction context and under the hindsight-conditioned context. The signed token-level log-probability gap determines update direction and local revision strength. TRIAL then aggregates absolute gaps within each turn and compares a turn’s share of total revision magnitude with its share of eligible tokens. This yields a trajectory-relative allocation multiplier. The multipliers are normalized so their eligible-token-weighted mean is one, which redistributes hindsight supervision while keeping the average allocation fixed. A scalar clamp bounds the dense loss contribution relative to the detached GRPO loss magnitude. Hindsight contexts, discrepancy scores, and allocation profiles are used only during training, so inference uses the ordinary policy.35:T48c,Across a nine-point Muon sweep on (a+b) mod 113, every run grokked and every run subsequently lost generalization; in the worst cases test accuracy fell below 1% and then partially recovered, repeatedly, over hundreds of thousands of steps. AdamW was not immune: four of seven AdamW configurations that grokked also lost generalization afterward, and severity increased monotonically with learning rate, from zero sub-threshold evaluations at 1e-3 to 918 at 1e-2. Unlike previously reported anti-grokking, here training accuracy fell alongside test accuracy, dropping from 100% to 21.12% in the same evaluation window while training loss rose from 1.53e-7 to 57.5, indicating that the learned function itself broke rather than merely failing to generalize. Ablating Muon’s normalized-orthogonalization path collapsed dispersion over the non-constant spectrum from 326 effective conjugate pairs to 4 while leaving the Fourier family the model computed through unchanged. Instability increased with depth; sweeping the output-head learning rate over a factor of five and weight decay from zero to 0.5 changed timing and severity but did not eliminate the collapse.36:T4aa,Decomposes a single-layer self-attention model into two linear circuits: the query-key circuit (W_K W_Q^T, which controls the attention distribution) and the output-value circuit (W_O W_V, which maps attended representations to prediction logits). Four parameterizations are compared: FAFO (both circuits factorized), CAFO (collapsed attention, factorized output), FACO (factorized attention, collapsed output), CACO (both collapsed). Gradient-flow analysis shows that factorized parameterizations induce a state-dependent rescaling of gradient updates in the corresponding collapsed parameter space, so factorized and collapsed models follow different optimization trajectories even when they reach similar losses. Under orthogonality and symmetry assumptions, closed-form population gradient-flow dynamics are derived: the output-value scalar grows as log(t), while the query-key scalar grows as log^2(t), with constants controlled by the learning-rate ratio η_QK / η_OV. Because the query-key circuit acts on attention through a saturating nonlinearity (softmax), this difference in growth rate directly explains how relative optimization speed drives attention sharpening during training.37:T704,The method has two main pieces: FFD for data construction and TACT for post-training.

FFD, or Factuality-Frequency Distillation, builds a text-anchored corpus of counter-commonsense QA pairs. The central idea is to estimate the prior strength of facts through frequency signals, then route examples according to how strongly they are supported by common language priors. Instead of generic synthetic QA generation, the pipeline focuses on informative low-frequency factual scenarios that are likely to trigger prior-over-evidence failures. This gives the training set a very specific role: it is designed to expose and stress the decoder’s tendency to over-trust dominant priors.

TACT, or Text-Anchored Cross-Modal Transfer, then uses this corpus to reshape the decoder. A key step is prior-aware trajectory curation: the method distinguishes reasoning traces that genuinely follow evidence from traces that merely rationalize the prior. The model is optimized in two stages. The first stage is supervised and reinforces a behavior pattern of grounding observations before making the final judgment. The second stage uses preference-style optimization to suppress prior-driven and over-rationalized trajectories. The paper’s central bet is that because the decoder is shared across modalities, text-only supervision can alter multimodal conflict resolution behavior.

This differs from prior work in a clean way. Methods such as VCD, VDD, M3ID, and MemVR mainly strengthen visual evidence or decoding from the visual side. NoLan-style methods calibrate inference-time behavior. TACT instead targets training-time decoder policy, and it does so with text-anchored supervision rather than extra visual labels. In practical terms, it is trying to teach the model to trust what it already sees.38:T58c,The most informative result is the diagnosis experiment. On CDH-Bench, the authors evaluate three MLLMs with multi-trajectory decoding using n=8 samples at temperature 1.0, and report both pass@k and a custom prior-lock@k metric. As k increases from 1 to 8, pass@k improves by 15–22 points while prior-lock@k drops by 14–18 points across models. This is strong evidence that the correct answer is often present in the decoding space but suppressed by prior-biased selection. Their error analysis on Qwen3-VL-8B points in the same direction: many failures choose the commonsense option, and a simple grounding prompt that asks the model to first describe the queried visual detail reduces such errors noticeably.

The main benchmark results show that TACT consistently improves visual counter-commonsense reasoning across multiple benchmarks while largely preserving general capabilities. The notable part is that these gains come from text-only supervision. The ablations indicate that the full pipeline matters: frequency-aware data construction, trajectory curation, and two-stage post-training each contribute. Plain answer-level SFT is weaker than supervision built around evidence-following trajectories. Another useful observation is the analysis of over-rationalization: longer CoT does not automatically help, because the model can generate visually phrased but incorrect explanations that justify the prior.39:T6c0,The main proposal is column-tiled SVD. Instead of applying one global SVD to a linear map W, the input columns are partitioned into tiles and each tile is factorized separately. Each extracted mode is a mount (v, u, sigma), interpreted as trigger, write, and strength. The identity of a mechanism is the weight rule itself, not a post hoc semantic label.

The more interesting contribution is the evaluation stack. The paper argues that tile-local lift is a misleading metric because one-column tiles win almost by construction. So the authors evaluate mounts using full-write energy lift on real forwards: can the extracted directions explain the site’s actual write tensor, not just local tile output? They also measure weight coverage, sparse write coverage, and coverage lift over random dictionaries, plus a saturation criterion that checks whether a small number of modes already captures most of the useful signal. For residual-write sites only, they add a causal check: inject the write direction after the post-sublayer RMSNorm so it lands in the residual stream, then compare the resulting logit changes against an unembedding-based prediction using Spearman correlation and top-20 Jaccard.

A key practical point is the use of effective-path mounts. Raw mlp.up and attn.v weights fail the residual-shaped evaluation because the on-distribution computation is not the raw matrix alone. For mlp.up, the paper uses a gate-averaged effective map W* = diag(g_bar) W_up. For attn.v, it fits a ridge least-squares map to mixed-v, with a fallback involving W_o. This is an important methodological claim: if the interpreted object does not match the effective computation path, even a good decomposition will look unusable.3a:T6d1,The experiments use Gemma-2-2B with 26 layers and seven linear maps per layer, for 182 site-layers in total. The corpus is built from WikiText-2 raw train: 86,109 tokens are collected and 16,384 are subsampled for analysis. Default tile widths are site-specific: 512 for residual and MLP maps, 256 for attn.k and attn.v, and 128 for attn.q, with a 64 fallback on hard layers. Experiments A and C use k=2 modes per tile, while experiment B sweeps k in {1, 2, 4, 8, 16}.

The headline result is that, with site-aware tiling and effective-path handling, all 182 site-layers pass the paper’s acceptance criteria: 52/52 for residual-write sites under A/B/C and 130/130 for the other sites under A/B. On the two residual-write sites, tiled SVD beats whole-matrix SVD, column sampling, and random baselines at nearly every depth, except mlp.down layer 25 where tiled and whole SVD are roughly tied but still pass under the ratio criterion. The paper also notes that column sampling can achieve tile-local lift around 0.999, which is precisely why local lift is excluded from the main judgment.

The coverage experiments show early saturation: on residual-write sites, coverage lift is already high at one or two modes per tile and then stays flat, suggesting that extra modes mostly add redundancy. For the steering check, the authors inject alpha*u with alpha=2 after the post-attention or post-FF RMSNorm, average last-token logit changes over up to eight texts, and compare them to the unembed-lens prediction. Passing requires either Spearman rho >= 0.05 or Jaccard@20 >= 0.05 for layers 6 and above. The overall empirical claim is that local column structure captures high-energy on-distribution write directions better than a single global SVD.3b:T555,Read the introduction first to understand the target of the critique: the paper is not dismissing SAE-style feature dictionaries, but arguing that they live in proxy space rather than weight space. Then go straight to the method section and focus on three things: the definition of a mount, the A/B/C evaluation criteria, and the effective-path treatment for mlp.up and attn.v. Those pieces determine whether the proposal is meaningful.

In the experiments, the most important comparison is tiled SVD versus whole-matrix SVD and column sampling on residual-write sites. Then look at the saturation results, because they support the claim that only a small number of modes per tile is needed. Finally, inspect the steering setup carefully: the injection happens after post-norm residual entry, not by directly perturbing o_proj or down_proj outputs, because the paper wants to test whether the direction functions as a residual write that matters for final logits.

A quick pass takes about 40 minutes. A deeper read is only worthwhile if you care about mechanistic analysis methodology or want to reuse the evaluation stack. One caveat is that the evidence is concentrated on a single model, Gemma-2-2B, and a small WikiText-2 subsample, with fairly hand-designed thresholds and acceptance rules. Generalization to larger models or other architectures remains open.3c:T41e,WebGrader frames the problem as executable reward construction. Given a website requirement, it derives required interaction flows and represents them as Flow Contracts.
